[0m[1m[38;5;9merror[vulnerability][0m[1m: Stack overflow in `Url::origin()` on nested `blob:` URLs[0m
    [0m[36m┌─[0m /home/runner/work/chump/chump/Cargo.lock:627:1
    [0m[36m│[0m
[0m[36m627[0m [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31murl 2.5.8 registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31m━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━[0m [0m[31msecurity vulnerability detected[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m
    [0m[36m├[0m ID: RUSTSEC-2026-0592
    [0m[36m├[0m Advisory: https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0592
    [0m[36m├[0m In `url` 2.5.8 and earlier, `Url::origin()` handled a `blob:` URL by parsing its path as another URL and calling itself on the result. It did so regardless of the inner URL's scheme, `blob` included. Each `blob:` prefix cost one stack frame, with no limit on nesting depth. `Url::parse()` accepts a URL with 10,000 `blob:` prefixes followed by `https://example.com/` (50,020 bytes in total); calling `Url::origin()` on it overflowed the default 2 MiB stack of a spawned thread.
      
      A stack overflow aborts the whole process, and `catch_unwind()` does not catch it. One such URL crashes any program that calls `Url::origin()` on untrusted input. `url::quirks::origin()` also calls `Url::origin()`. For example, Servo's `URL.origin` DOM getter calls `url::quirks::origin()`, so a script on a page can trigger the overflow.
      
      `url` 2.5.9 follows the URL Standard: `Url::origin()` takes the origin of the inner URL only when its scheme is `http`, `https`, or `file`, returns an opaque origin otherwise, and never calls itself. A `blob:` URL with another inner scheme that previously produced a tuple origin, such as `blob:ws://...` or `blob:blob:https://...`, now returns an opaque origin.
    [0m[36m├[0m Announcement: https://github.com/servo/rust-url/pull/1186
    [0m[36m├[0m Solution: Upgrade to >=2.5.9 (try `cargo update -p url`)
